.
We start by proving some intermediate results that play the crucial role in the proof (
Section

F-B1
).

F-B
1
Intermediate Results

First, we show that a correct server that updates its
\mathit{current\_view}.\mathit{view}
variable to
v
has
v
in its
\mathit{history}
variable.

Lemma 13
.

Let
\mathit{current\_view}.\mathit{view}=v
at a correct server
r
at time
t
.
Then,
v\in\mathit{history}
at server
r
at time
t
.

Proof.

If
v=\mathit{genesis}
, the lemma follows from line
6
of
and the fact that no view is ever removed from the
\mathit{history}
variable of a correct server.

Let
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
.
Since
\mathit{current\_view}.\mathit{view}=v
at server
r
at time
t
and
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
, we know that line
28
of
is executed by
r
at time
t_{v}\leq t
.
Hence,
\mathit{reconfiguration}.\mathit{destination}=v
at time
t_{v}
.
Moreover,
v\in\mathit{history}
at time
t_{v}
at server
r
(by line
3
of
and the fact that no view is ever removed from the
\mathit{history}
variable of a correct server).
Therefore, the lemma holds in this case, as well.
∎

Next, we show that, if
v\in\mathit{history}
at a correct server, then the server has previously obtained a view-path to
v
.

Lemma 14
.

Let
v\in\mathit{history}
at a correct server
r
at time
t
, where
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
.
Then, there exists a view-path
\mathit{path}=[m_{1},...,m_{k}]
such that (1)
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination}()=v
, and (2)
m_{i}\in\mathit{install\_messages}
at server
r
at time
t
, for every
i\in[1,k]
.

Proof.

In order to prove the lemma, we show that, if, at any point in time,
v^{*}\in\mathit{history}
at a correct server
r
, then
r
has previously obtained a view-path to
v^{*}
(and the view-path consists of messages from the
\mathit{install\_messages}
variable).
Note that no message is ever removed from the
\mathit{install\_messages}
variable of a correct server.
We set the following invariant to hold at time
t^{*}\geq 0
: If
v^{*}\in\mathit{history}
at time
t^{*}
, then
r
has previously included a view-path to
v^{*}
in
\mathit{install\_messages}
.
Observe that the invariant holds initially (i.e., at the time the server starts) since
\mathit{history}=\{\mathit{genesis}\}
(line
6
of
) and
\mathit{install\_messages}=\emptyset
(line
7
of
).

We now prove that the invariant is preserved once the
\mathit{history}
variable at server
r
is updated with a view
v^{**}
.
The only place in which the
\mathit{history}
variable can be modified is line
11
of
.
Since
v\in\mathit{history}
(by line
6
of
), we know that there exists a view-path to
v
, which is previously obtained and included in
\mathit{install\_messages}
(because of the invariant hypothesis).
Hence, there is a view-path to
v^{**}
since the received
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message is included in
\mathit{install\_messages}
(line
8
of
).
Thus, the lemma holds.
∎

Lemma

15
proves that any view
v
that belongs to the
\mathit{history}
variable of a correct server is valid.

Lemma 15
.

Let
v\in\mathit{history}
at a correct server.
Then,
v
is a valid view.

Proof.

If
v=\mathit{genesis}
,
Definition

1
is satisfied for
v
.
Otherwise,
Definition

1
holds for
v
because of
Lemma

14
.
∎

Finally, we show that correct servers “transit” only to valid views, i.e., if
\mathit{current\_view}.\mathit{view}=v
at a correct server, then
v
is a valid view.

Lemma 16
.

Let
\mathit{current\_view}.\mathit{view}=v
at a correct server
r
at some time
t\geq 0
.
Then,
v
is a valid view.

Proof.

We know that
v\in\mathit{history}
at server
r
(by
Lemma

13
).
Hence,
v
is valid by
Lemma

15
.
∎

We now give a brief explanation of the upcoming steps in proving the properties from
.

Explanation.

We fix some time
t
in an execution of Carbon.
At time
t
, we observe all
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages obtained by (correct or faulty) processes.
Then, we introduce a set of invariants concerned with all obtained
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages (this set of invariants is satisfied at time
0
).
The introduced invariants allow us to identify all possible “new”
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages that can be obtained after time
t
.
Finally, we prove that the invariants are preserved after such
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages are indeed obtained by processes after time
t
.

Fix a time
t\geq 0
in an execution.
Let
\mathcal{I}_{p}(t)
denote all
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages obtained by a (correct or faulty) process
p
by time
t
; note that obtaining of an
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message is irrevocable, i.e.,
\mathcal{I}_{p}(t^{\prime})\subseteq\mathcal{I}_{p}(t)
, for any time
t^{\prime}\leq t
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{I}_{p}(0)=\emptyset
, for every process
p
.
Let
\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)=\bigcup\limits_{p\in\mathcal{C}\cup\mathcal{R}}\mathcal{I}_{p}(t)
.
Observe that
\mathcal{I}^{*}(t^{\prime})\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
, for any time
t^{\prime}<t
.

We say that a view
v
is
well-founded
at time
t
if and only if:

•

v=\mathit{genesis}
, or

•

there exists a view-path
\mathit{path}
such that
\mathit{path}\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
and
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination()}=v
.

Now, we prove that, if a process has obtained an
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message (see
) associated with a view
\mathit{source}
by time
t
and
\mathit{source}
is not well-founded at time
t
, then
\mathit{source}
is not a valid view.

Lemma 17
.

Let a (correct or faulty) process obtain
m=[\mathtt{INSTALL},\mathit{source},\mathit{set},\omega]
at some time
t>0
.
Moreover, let
\mathit{source}
not be well-founded at time
t-1
.
Then,
\mathit{source}
is not a valid view.

Proof.

Note that
\mathit{source}\neq\mathit{genesis}
, since
\mathit{genesis}
is well-founded at any time.
We prove the lemma by contradiction.
Hence, let
\mathit{source}
be a valid view.

Since
\mathit{source}
is a valid view, that means that (at least) a quorum of members of
\mathit{source}
are correct (by the failure model).
Moreover, we know that
\mathit{set}
is committed by
\mathit{vg}(\mathit{source})
(by
).
Because of the fact that
\omega
contains messages from at least
\mathit{source}.\mathtt{quorum()}
distinct members of
\mathit{source}
(see
), there exists a correct server
r
that sets its
\mathit{current\_view}.\mathit{view}
variable to
\mathit{source}
before time
t
at line
28
of
(since
\mathit{source}\neq\mathit{genesis}
and
\mathit{vg}(\mathit{source})
is started by
r
, which follows from the decision permission property of
\mathit{vg}(\mathit{source})
).
By
Lemma

13
,
\mathit{source}\in\mathit{history}
before time
t
.
Furthermore,
Lemma

14
shows that
r
has obtained a view-path to
\mathit{source}
before time
t
.
Therefore,
\mathit{source}
is well-founded at time
t-1
, which implies contradiction.
∎

Finally, we introduce a few concepts that describe the state of the system at some fixed time
t
.
First, we denote by
V(t)
the set of well-founded views at time
t
, i.e.,
V(t)=\{v\,|\,v\text{ is well-founded at time }t\}
.

For every view
v\in V(t)
, we define a logical predicate
\alpha_{t}(v)
such that
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
if and only of:

•

v=\mathit{genesis}
, or

•

[\mathtt{INSTALL},v^{\prime},\{v\},\omega]\in\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
, where
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
; in other words,
m\in\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
, where
m.\mathtt{source()}\in V(t)
,
m.\mathtt{destination()}=v
and
m.\mathtt{tail()}=\emptyset
.

Otherwise,
\alpha_{t}(v)=\bot
.
If
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
, we say that
v
is
installable
at time
t
.

Moreover, for every view
v\in V(t)
, we define
\beta_{t}(v)
such that
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v)
if and only if
m=[\mathtt{INSTALL},v^{\prime},\mathit{set}^{\prime}=\{v\}\cup\mathit{set},\omega]\in\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
, where
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
,
m.\mathtt{destination()}=v
, and
\mathit{set}\neq\emptyset
;
in other words,
m\in\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
, where
m.\mathtt{source()}\in V(t)
,
m.\mathtt{destination()}=v
and
m.\mathtt{tail()}=\mathit{set}\neq\emptyset
.
Otherwise,
\beta_{t}(v)=\emptyset
.

Finally, for every view
v\in V(t)
, we define
\rho_{t}(v)
such that
\mathit{set}\in\rho_{t}(v)
if and only if
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{set},\omega]\in\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
.

First, we prove that every view
v\in V(t)
is valid.

Lemma 18
.

Let
v\in V(t)
.
Then,
v
is a valid view.

Proof.

Follows from the definition of well-founded views and
Definition

1
.
∎

We now introduce the first invariant.

Invariant 1
.

Let
v\in V(t)
.
If
\mathit{set}\in\rho_{t}(v)
, then (1)
\mathit{set}\neq\emptyset
, (2)
\mathit{set}
is a sequence, and (3)
\mathit{set}.\mathtt{follows}(v)=\top
.

Since
\mathcal{I}^{*}(0)=\emptyset
, we conclude that
V(0)=\{\mathit{genesis}\}
and
1
holds at time
0
.
Now, we show that any information obtained by a process by time
t
is captured in
V(t)
,
\rho_{t}
and
\beta_{t}
.

Lemma 19
.

Let a (correct or faulty) process
p
obtain a view-path
\mathit{path}=[m_{1},m_{2},...,m_{k}]
by time
t
, where
k\geq 1
.
Then, the following holds:

•

\mathit{path}.\mathtt{views}()\subseteq V(t)
;

•

Let
\mathit{destination}=\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination}()
.
If
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{tail}()\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{tail}()\in\beta_{t}(\mathit{destination})
.

Proof.

Since
p
obtains
\mathit{path}
by time
t
, we conclude that
m_{i}\in\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
, for every
i\in[1,k]
.
We know that
\mathit{genesis}\in V(t)
, by the definition of well-founded views.
All other views that belong to
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{views}()
, therefore, are included in
V(t)
(including
\mathit{destination}
and
m_{k}.\mathtt{source}()
).
Hence, if
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{tail}()\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{tail}()\in\beta_{t}(\mathit{destination})
.
∎

Next, we show that any two sets that belong to
\rho_{t}(v)
, where
v\in V(t)
, are “comparable”.

Lemma 20
.

Let
v\in V(t)
and let
\mathit{set}_{1},\mathit{set}_{2}\in\rho_{t}(v)
.
Then, either
\mathit{set}_{1}\subseteq\mathit{set}_{2}
or
\mathit{set}_{1}\supset\mathit{set}_{2}
.

Proof.

Since
v\in V(t)
, we know that
v
is a valid view (by
Lemma

18
).
Moreover, we know that
\mathit{set}_{1}
and
\mathit{set}_{2}
are committed by
\mathit{vg}(v)
(by
).
Hence, the lemma follows from the comparability property of
\mathit{vg}(v)
.
∎

We now define an invariant that explains how installable views are “instantiated”.

Invariant 2

(Creation of Installable Views)
.

For every
v\in V(t)
such that (1)
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
, and (2)
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
, there exists a view
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
such that:

•

\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v,(x\geq 0)
,
11
11

11

Recall that all sets that belong to
\rho_{t}(v)
, for any view
v\in V(t)
, are sequences (by
1
).
and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v
.

Note that
2
is satisfied at time
0
since
V(0)=\{\mathit{genesis}\}
.
Next, we define what it means for a view to
lead
to another view.

Definition 3

(Leading to a View)
.

Consider views
v,v^{\prime}\in V(t)
such that:

•

\alpha_{t}(v)=\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v,(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v
.

We say that
v^{\prime}

leads to

v
at time
t
.

We now show that
v^{\prime}\subset v
if
v^{\prime}
leads to
v
.

Lemma 21
.

Let
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
lead to
v\in V(t)
at time
t
.
Then,
v^{\prime}\subset v
.

Proof.

By
Definition

3
,
\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}=...\to v
.
Since
\mathit{seq}.\mathtt{follows}(v^{\prime})=\top
(by
1
), the lemma holds.
∎

Now, we introduce another invariant that we assume holds at time
t
:
\mathit{genesis}
is a subset of every other installable view.

Invariant 3
.

For every view
v\in V(t)
such that (1)
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
, and (2)
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
,
\mathit{genesis}\subset v
.

3
holds at time
0
since
V(0)=\{\mathit{genesis}\}
.
Now, we show that, for every installable view different from
\mathit{genesis}
, there exists a view that leads to it at time
t
(follows directly from
2
).

Lemma 22
.

Let
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
such that (1)
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and (2)
v^{\prime}\neq\mathit{genesis}
.
Then, there exists a view
v\in V(t)
such that
v
leads to
v^{\prime}
at time
t
.

Proof.

According to
2
, there exists a view
v\in V(t)
such that:

•

\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime}
.

By
Definition

3
,
v
leads to
v^{\prime}
at time
t
, which concludes the proof.
∎

We now prove that no view leads to
\mathit{genesis}
at time
t
.

Lemma 23
.

No view
v\in V(t)
leads to
\mathit{genesis}
at time
t
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma by contradiction.
Let there exist a view
v\in V(t)
such that
v
leads to
\mathit{genesis}
at time
t
.
By
Lemma

21
,
v\subset\mathit{genesis}
.
However, this contradicts
3
, which concludes the proof.
∎

A view can lead to (at most) one other view.
The following lemma proves this statement.

Lemma 24
.

Let
v\in V(t)
such that
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.
If
v
leads to
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
and
v
leads to
v^{\prime\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
, then
v^{\prime}=v^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

According to
Definition

3
,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}
is a sequence (by
1
) and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=...\to v^{\prime}
.
Similarly,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
is a sequence (by
1
) and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}=...\to v^{\prime\prime}
.
According to
Lemma

20
, either
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
or
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\supset\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
.
Let us analyze two possible cases:

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
: In this case, we have that
v^{\prime}=v^{\prime\prime}
and the lemma holds.

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\neq\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
: Without loss of generality, let
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\subset\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
.
Therefore,
v^{\prime}\in\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
and
v^{\prime}\subseteq v^{\prime\prime}
.
If
v^{\prime}\subset v^{\prime\prime}
,
Definition

3
is not satisfied for
v^{\prime\prime}
(since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
).
Hence,
v^{\prime}=v^{\prime\prime}
.

The lemma holds.
∎

Now, we prove that there exists exactly one view that does not lead to any view.
Recall that we assume that only finitely many valid views exist (see
Appendix

F
, paragraph “Failure model & assumptions”).
Since all views that belong to
V(t)
are valid (by
Lemma

18
), we conclude that
|V(t)|<\infty
.

Lemma 25
.

There exists
exactly
one view
v\in V(t)
such that (1)
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
, and (2)
v
does not lead to any view at time
t
.

Proof.

Let there be
x
installable views at time
t
.
Because of
Lemmas

22
and
23
, we conclude that
exactly

x-1
installable views (all installable views except
\mathit{genesis}
;
\mathit{genesis}
is installable at any time) have views that lead to them at time
t
.
According to
Lemma

24
, each installable view leads to at most one view at time
t
.
Hence, at least
x-1
views lead to a view at time
t
.

In order to prove the lemma, it is sufficient to show that it is impossible for all
x
installable views to lead to some view at time
t
.
We prove this statement by contradiction.

Let
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
denote the set of all installable views at time
t
; note that
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}|<\infty
(since
|V(t)|<\infty
and
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}\subseteq V(t)
).
Consider the following construction:

1.

Start with
V\leftarrow\{\mathit{genesis}\}
,
\mathit{last}\leftarrow\mathit{genesis}
and
R\leftarrow\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}\setminus\{\mathit{genesis}\}
.

2.

Repeat until
V\neq\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
:

(a)

Select a view
v\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
such that
\mathit{last}
leads to
v
at time
t
.

(b)

Update
V\leftarrow V\cup\{v\}
,
\mathit{last}\leftarrow v
and
R\leftarrow R\setminus\{v\}
.

(c)

If
V\neq\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
, go to step 2.

(d)

Otherwise, select a view
v\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
such that
\mathit{last}
leads to
v
at time
t
.

Note that
V
represents a sequence (because of the construction and
Lemma

21
).
Hence,
\mathit{last}
represents the greatest (with respect to the containment relation) element of
V
.
Therefore, a view
v
, where
\mathit{last}
leads to
v
at time
t
, must belong to
R
(otherwise, we would contradict
Lemma

21
).
Thus, the
V
set is constantly “growing” and the
R
set is constantly “shrinking”.

Once
V=\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
in step 2(c), we conclude that
R=\emptyset
.
Hence,
\mathit{last}
cannot lead to any view.
Thus, we reach contradiction with the fact that
\mathit{last}
leads to a view, which concludes the proof.
∎

The next lemma shows that at most one view can lead to a view
v
at time
t
.

Lemma 26
.

Let
v\in V(t)
such that
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.
If
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
leads to
v
at time
t
and
v^{\prime\prime}\in V(t)
leads to
v
at time
t
, then
v^{\prime}=v^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

Let there be
x
installable views at time
t
.
Because of
Lemmas

22
and
23
, we conclude that exactly
x-1
installable views (all installable views except
\mathit{genesis}
) have views that lead to them.
According to
Lemma

24
, each view leads to at most one view.

Lemma

25
shows that there exists an installable view that does not lead to any view at time
t
.
Hence, there are exactly
x-1
installable views and exactly
x-1
“lead-to” relations.
Thus, only one view leads to
v
at time
t
and the lemma holds.
∎

Finally, we show that all installable views are comparable.

Lemma 27
.

Let
v,v^{\prime}\in V(t)
such that
\alpha_{t}(v)=\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
.
Then, either
v\subseteq v^{\prime}
or
v\supset v^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
denote the set of all installable views at time
t
.
In order to prove the lemma, we prove that
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
is a sequence.
Let
x=|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}|<\infty
.
According to
Lemma

25
, exactly
x-1
installable views lead to some view at time
t
.

Consider the construction similar to the one from the proof of
Lemma

25
:

1.

Start with
V\leftarrow\{\mathit{genesis}\}
and
\mathit{last}\leftarrow\mathit{genesis}
.

2.

Repeat until
\mathit{last}
does not lead to any view at time
t
:

(a)

Select a view
v\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
such that
\mathit{last}
leads to
v
at time
t
.

(b)

Update
V\leftarrow V\cup\{v\}
and
\mathit{last}\leftarrow v
.

Because of the construction and
Lemma

21
,
V
is a sequence.
In order to conclude the lemma, it suffices to prove that
V=\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
.
We prove this statement by contradiction.

Suppose that
V\neq\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
.
Because of the construction, we know that
V\subset\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
.
Let
|V|=p<x
.
This means that there exist
x-p
views in
R=\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}\setminus{V}
.
The following holds for each view
v\in R
:

•

v
leads to a view
v^{\prime}\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
at time
t
:
This statement holds because
x-1
installable views lead to some view at time
t
(by
Lemma

25
).
By construction, we have that
p-1
views of
V
lead to some view at time
t
.
Given that
|R|=x-p
and
x-1-(p-1)=x-p
, the statement is true.

•

v
leads to a view
v^{\prime}\in R
at time
t
:
Suppose that
v
leads to a view
v^{\prime}\in V
at time
t
.
If
v^{\prime}=\mathit{genesis}
,
Lemma

23
is contradicted.
If
v^{\prime}\neq\mathit{genesis}
,
Lemma

26
is contradicted (since
v^{\prime}
has more than one view leading to it).
Thus, the statement is correct.

Finally, we model the set
R
with a graph
G
with
x-p
vertices (
x-p
installable views) and
x-p
edges (
x-p
“lead-to” relations).
Thus,
G
has a cycle.
However, that is not possible given
Lemma

21
.
Hence,
V=\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
and the lemma holds.
∎

The next lemma proves that only the greatest installable view at time
t
does not lead to any view at time
t
.
Recall that all installable views are comparable (by
Lemma

27
).

Lemma 28
.

A view
v\in V(t)
, where
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
, does not lead to any view at time
t
if and only if
v
is the greatest installable view at time
t
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma by proving both directions of the statement.

Let
v
be the view that does not lead to any view at time
t
; such view exists due to
Lemma

25
.
By contradiction, suppose that
v
is not the greatest installable view at time
t
.
Let
v_{\mathit{max}}
be the greatest installable view at time
t
.
According to
Lemma

25
,
v_{\mathit{max}}
leads to some view
v^{\prime}
at time
t
.
By
Lemma

21
, we have that
v_{\mathit{max}}\subset v^{\prime}
, which contradicts the fact that
v_{\mathit{max}}
is the greatest installable view at time
t
.
Thus, the lemma holds in this direction.

Suppose that
v
is the greatest installable view at time
t
and that, by contradiction, it leads to a view
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
.
According to
Lemma

21
,
v\subset v^{\prime}
.
This is contradiction with the fact that
v
is the greatest installable view at time
t
.
The lemma holds in this direction, as well.
∎

Now, we introduce an invariant that explains how non-installable views are “instantiated”.

Invariant 4

(Creation of Non-Installable Views)
.

For every
v\in V(t)
such that
\alpha_{t}(v)=\bot
, there exists a view
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
such that:

•

\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0,y\geq 1)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

4
holds at time
0
since
V(0)=\{\mathit{genesis}\}
and
\alpha_{0}(\mathit{genesis})=\top
.
Next, we define
auxiliary views
.

Definition 4

(Auxiliary View)
.

Consider views
v,v^{\prime}\in V(t)
such that:

•

\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and

•

\alpha_{t}(v)=\bot
, and

•

\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0,y\geq 1)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

We say that
v
is an
auxiliary view
for
v^{\prime}
at time
t
.

The following lemma shows that any non-installable view is an auxiliary view for some installable view.

Lemma 29
.

Let
v\in V(t)
such that
\alpha_{t}(v)=\bot
.
Then,
v
is an auxiliary view for a view
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
.

Proof.

The lemma follows from
4
and
Definition

4
.
∎

If
v
is an auxiliary view for
v^{\prime}
at time
t
, then
v^{\prime}\subset v
.
The following lemma proves this claim.

Lemma 30
.

Let
v\in V(t)
be an auxiliary view for a view
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
.
Then,
v^{\prime}\subset v
.

Proof.

According to
Definition

4
,
\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}=...\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(y\geq 1)
.
By
1
, we know that
\mathit{seq}.\mathtt{follows}(v^{\prime})=\top
, which implies that
v^{\prime}\subset v
.
∎

If
v
is an auxiliary view for a view
v^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
leads to a view
v^{\prime\prime}
, then
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
.

Lemma 31
.

Let
v\in V(t)
be an auxiliary view for a view
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
.
Let
v^{\prime}
lead to
v^{\prime\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
.
Then,
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

According to
Definition

4
,
\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}=...\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(y\geq 1)
.
By
Definition

3
,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}=...\to v^{\prime\prime}
.

1
ensures that (1)
\mathit{seq}
and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
are sequences, and (2)
\mathit{seq}.\mathtt{follows}(v^{\prime})=\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}.\mathtt{follows}(v^{\prime})=\top
.
By
Lemma

20
, there are three possible scenarios:

•

\mathit{seq}=\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
: In this case, we have that
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
.
The lemma holds.

•

\mathit{seq}\subset\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
: In this case, we know that
v\in\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
.
Thus,
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
and the lemma holds.

•

\mathit{seq}\supset\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
: Hence,
v^{\prime\prime}\in\mathit{seq}
.
We conclude that
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
because, otherwise, either (1)
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
(if
v=v^{\prime\prime}
), or (2)
v
is not an auxiliary view for
v^{\prime}
at time
t
since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime\prime})=\top
(if
v^{\prime\prime}\subset v
).

The lemma holds since the claim is correct in all possible cases.
∎

The following lemma shows that if
v
leads to
v^{\prime}
, then there does not exist an installable view
v^{\prime\prime}
such that
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}\subset v^{\prime}
.

Lemma 32
.

Let
v\in V(t)
lead to
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
.
There does not exist a view
v^{\prime\prime}\in V(t)
such that (1)
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime\prime})=\top
, and (2)
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}\subset v^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}=\{v\,|\,v\in V(t)\land\alpha_{t}(v)=\top\}
.
According to
Lemma

27
,
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
is a sequence.
Moreover,
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}|<\infty
.

We prove the lemma by contradiction.
Hence, suppose that there exists a view
v^{\prime\prime}
such that (1)
v^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
, and (2)
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}\subset v^{\prime}
.

Since
v
leads to
v^{\prime}
at time
t
, we know that
v\subset v^{\prime}
(by
Lemma

21
).
We consider the following construction:

1.

Start with
V\leftarrow\{v,v^{\prime}\}
and
\mathit{first}\leftarrow v
.

2.

Repeat until
\mathit{first}=\mathit{genesis}
:

(a)

Select a view
v_{i}\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
such that
v_{i}
leads to
\mathit{first}
.

(b)

Update
V\leftarrow V\cup\{v_{i}\}
and
\mathit{first}\leftarrow v_{i}
.

According to the construction and
Lemma

21
,
V
is a sequence.
Moreover,
\mathit{first}
represents the first (i.e., smallest) view of
V
.
Therefore, a view
v_{i}
, where
v_{i}
leads to
\mathit{first}
, must belong to
\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}\setminus{V}
(otherwise, we would contradict
Lemma

21
).
Thus,
V
is “growing” with each iteration.
Finally, an execution of the construction eventually terminates since (1)
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}|<\infty
, (2)
Lemma

22
holds, (3)
Lemma

23
stands, and (4)
\mathit{genesis}\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
.

Since
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
, we conclude that
v^{\prime\prime}\notin V
and
v^{\prime\prime}\neq\mathit{genesis}
.
Therefore, there exists a view that leads to
v^{\prime\prime}
(by
Lemma

22
).
Let
R=\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}\setminus{V}
.
Hence,
v^{\prime\prime}\in R
.
Now, we consider the following construction:

1.

Start with
V^{\prime}\leftarrow\{v^{\prime\prime}\}
and
\mathit{first}\leftarrow v^{\prime\prime}
.

2.

Select a view
v_{i}\in\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}
such that
v_{i}
leads to
\mathit{first}
.

3.

If
v_{i}\in V
, then terminate.

4.

Update
V^{\prime}\leftarrow V^{\prime}\cup\{v_{i}\}
and
\mathit{first}\leftarrow v_{i}
.

5.

Go to step 2.

By construction and
Lemma

21
,
V^{\prime}
is a sequence.
Hence, for every view
\mathit{view}\in V^{\prime}
,
\mathit{view}\subseteq v^{\prime\prime}
.
Importantly, an execution of this construction eventually terminates because (1)
|\mathcal{I}\mathcal{N}\mathcal{S}|<\infty
, (2)
Lemma

22
, (3)
Lemma

23
, and (4)
\mathit{genesis}\in V
.
Thus, once the execution terminates, we either contradict
Lemma

21
(if
v_{i}=v^{\prime}
; since
\mathit{view}\subseteq v^{\prime\prime}
, for every
\mathit{view}\in V^{\prime}
) or
Lemma

24
(if
v_{i}\neq v^{\prime}
).
Therefore, the lemma holds.
∎

Now, we prove that a view can be an auxiliary view for a single installable view.

Lemma 33
.

Let
v\in V(t)
be an auxiliary view for a view
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
.
Moreover, let
v
be an auxiliary view for a view
v^{\prime\prime}\in V(t)
at time
t
.
Then,
v^{\prime}=v^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

By contradiction, suppose that
v^{\prime}\neq v^{\prime\prime}
.

Lemma

27
shows that either
v^{\prime}\subset v^{\prime\prime}
or
v^{\prime}\supset v^{\prime\prime}
.
Without loss of generality, let
v^{\prime}\subset v^{\prime\prime}
.
Given
Lemma

30
, we conclude that
v^{\prime}\subset v
and
v^{\prime\prime}\subset v
.

Let
v^{\prime}
lead to a view
v_{i}\in V(t)
at time
t
(such view
v_{i}
exists since
v^{\prime}\subset v^{\prime\prime}
and
Lemmas

25
and
28
).
We know, by
Lemma

27
, that either
v_{i}\subseteq v^{\prime\prime}
or
v_{i}\supset v^{\prime\prime}
.
We conclude that
v_{i}\subseteq v^{\prime\prime}
(otherwise, the statement of
Lemma

32
would be violated).

Lemma

31
shows that
v\subset v_{i}
.
Given that
v_{i}\subseteq v^{\prime\prime}
, we conclude that
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
.
This represents contradiction with
v^{\prime\prime}\subset v
, which concludes the proof.
∎

The next lemma proves that, if a process obtains an evidence
\epsilon
for
\mathit{set}\neq\emptyset
and view
v^{\prime}
by time
t
, then
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v)
.

Lemma 34
.

Let a (correct or faulty) process obtain an evidence
\epsilon^{\prime}
by time
t
such that
\mathtt{valid}((v^{\prime},\mathit{set}^{\prime},\epsilon^{\prime}),v)=\top
, where
\mathit{set}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
.
Then,
v\in V(t)
and
\mathit{set}^{\prime}\in\beta_{t}(v)
.

Proof.

First, we conclude that
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
since
\mathtt{valid}((v^{\prime},\mathit{set}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset,\epsilon^{\prime}),\mathit{genesis})
must return
\bot
(because of the check at line
3
of
).
Moreover, we know that
\epsilon^{\prime}.\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination()}=v
and
\epsilon^{\prime}.\mathit{path}.\mathtt{tail()}=\mathit{set}^{\prime}
(by the check at line
45
of
).
By
Lemma

19
, we know that
v\in V(t)
and
\mathit{set}^{\prime}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Similarly, if a process obtains an evidence
\epsilon
for
\mathit{set}=\emptyset
by time
t
, then
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.

Lemma 35
.

Let a (correct or faulty) process obtain an evidence
\epsilon^{\prime}
by time
t
such that
\mathtt{valid}((v^{\prime},\emptyset,\epsilon^{\prime}),v)=\top
,
Then,
v\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.

Proof.

If
v=\mathit{genesis}
, the lemma trivially holds.
Otherwise, we know that
\epsilon^{\prime}.\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination()}=v
and
\epsilon^{\prime}.\mathit{path}.\mathtt{tail()}=\emptyset
(by the check at line
45
of
).
By
Lemma

19
,
v\in V(t)
.
Let
m
be the last message in the ordered set of
\epsilon^{\prime}.\mathit{path}
.
We know that
m.\mathtt{tail()}=\emptyset
and
m.\mathtt{destination()}=v
(by
).
Moreover,
m\in\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
and
m.\mathtt{source()}\in V(t)
(by
Lemma

19
).
Hence,
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
, the lemma holds.
∎

We now introduce the last invariant we assume holds at time
t
.

Invariant 5
.

Let
v\in V(t)
.
If
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{set}=\{v_{1}^{\prime},...,v_{y}^{\prime}\}
and
y\geq 1
, then:

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
,
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

Since
\mathcal{I}^{*}(0)=\emptyset
at time
0
, we know that
V(0)=\{\mathit{genesis}\}
and
\beta_{0}(\mathit{genesis})=\emptyset
.
Hence,
5
is satisfied at time
0
.

Lemma 36
.

\beta_{t}(\mathit{genesis})=\emptyset
.

Proof.

By contradiction, let
\beta_{t}(\mathit{genesis})\neq\emptyset
.
Let
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(\mathit{genesis})
, where
\mathit{set}=\{v_{1}^{\prime},...,v_{y}^{\prime}\}
and
y\geq 1
.
According to
5
, there exists an installable view
v\in V(t)
such that
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to\mathit{genesis}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
and
x\geq 0
.
By
1
, we know that
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}.\mathtt{follows}(v)=\top
, which implies that
v\subset\mathit{genesis}
.
However, this statement contradicts
3
, which concludes the proof.
∎

We prove that, if
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, then
\mathit{set}
is a sequence and
\mathit{set}.\mathtt{follows}(v)=\top
.

Lemma 37
.

Let
v\in V(t)
.
If
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, then (1)
\mathit{set}
is a sequence, and (2)
\mathit{set}.\mathtt{follows}(v)=\top
.

Proof.

Let
\mathit{set}=\{v_{1}^{\prime},...,v_{y}^{\prime}\}
, where
y\geq 1
.
By
5
, there exists a view
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
such that (1)
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and (2)
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
, where
x\geq 0
.
Therefore,
\mathit{set}
is a sequence and
\mathit{set}.\mathtt{follows}(v)=\top
.
∎

The next lemma shows that, if
\mathit{seq}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, where
v
is an installable view, and
\mathit{seq}
“comes from” an installable view
v^{\prime}
, then
v^{\prime}
leads to
v
.
Recall that all sets in
\beta_{t}(v)
are sequences according to
Lemma

37
.

Lemma 38
.

Let
\mathit{seq}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(y\geq 1)
and
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.
Then,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
and
v^{\prime}
leads to
v
at time
t
.

Proof.

By
5
, we know that:

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

In order to conclude the proof, we need to prove that
v^{\prime}
indeed leads to
v
at time
t
.

By
1
, we have that
v^{\prime}\subset v
.
By contradiction, suppose that
v^{\prime}
leads to
v^{\prime\prime}\neq v
at time
t
(such view
v^{\prime\prime}
exists since
v^{\prime}\subset v
and
Lemmas

25
and
28
).
Therefore, by
Definition

3
,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}=...\to v^{\prime\prime}
.
Let us analyze all three possible cases according to
Lemma

20
:

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
: Hence,
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
and
Definition

3
is not satisfied for
v^{\prime\prime}
(since
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
).

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\subset\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
: Hence,
v\in\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
.
Thus,
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
and
Definition

3
is not satisfied for
v^{\prime\prime}
(since
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
).

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\supset\mathit{seq}^{\prime\prime}
: Hence,
v^{\prime\prime}\in\mathit{seq}^{\prime}
.
If
v^{\prime\prime}\subset v
,
5
is violated since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime\prime})=\top
.
If
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
, then we have the following: (1)
v^{\prime}
leads to
v^{\prime\prime}
, and (2)
v^{\prime}\subset v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
, where
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.
Hence,
Lemma

32
is contradicted.

The lemma holds.
∎

The next lemma shows that, if
\mathit{seq}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, where
v
is non-installable, and
\mathit{seq}
“comes from” an installable view
v^{\prime}
, then
v
is an auxiliary view for
v^{\prime}
.

Lemma 39
.

Let
\mathit{seq}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(y\geq 1)
and
\alpha_{t}(v)=\bot
.
Then,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, views from the
\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
set are not installable at time
t
and
v
is an auxiliary view for
v^{\prime}
at time
t
.

Proof.

By
5
, we know that:

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

Hence,
Definition

4
is satisfied and
v
is an auxiliary view for
v^{\prime}
at time
t
.
Moreover, views from the
\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
set are not installable at time
t
, which concludes the proof.
∎

The next lemma shows that, if
\mathit{seq}_{1},\mathit{seq}_{2}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, then
\mathit{seq}_{1}
and
\mathit{seq}_{2}
are comparable.

Lemma 40
.

Let
v\in V(t)
.
If
\mathit{seq}_{1},\mathit{seq}_{2}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, then either
\mathit{seq}_{1}\subseteq\mathit{seq}_{2}
or
\mathit{seq}_{1}\supset\mathit{seq}_{2}
.

Proof.

Let
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.
For every
\mathit{seq}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(y\geq 1)
,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=...\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}
leads to
v
at time
t
(according to
Lemma

38
).
By
Lemma

26
, only view
v^{\prime}
leads to
v
at time
t
.
Thus, the lemma follows from
Lemma

20
.

Let
\alpha_{t}(v)=\bot
.
For every
\mathit{seq}\in\beta_{t}(v)
, where
\mathit{seq}=v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(y\geq 1)
,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=...\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
and
v
is an auxiliary view for
v^{\prime}
at time
t
(according to
Lemma

39
).
According to
Lemma

33
,
v
is an auxiliary view only for view
v^{\prime}
at time
t
.
Thus, the lemma follows from
Lemma

20
.
∎

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{set},\omega]
be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
.
We prove that either
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v)
or
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.
Note that we do not consider
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages with
v\notin V(t)
since, in that case,
v
is not a valid view (by
Lemma

17
).

Importantly, if multiple new
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages are obtained at the same time
t^{\prime}>t
, the notion of the “first”
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message is not defined.
However, in that case, we order the messages in any arbitrary way.
In other words, we “artificially” select the “first”
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message and observe its properties, as well as the properties after this message has been processed (i.e., added to the set of all obtained
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages).
Importantly, we process all messages from time
t^{\prime}
before moving on to processing messages from some time
t^{\prime\prime}>t^{\prime}
.
We are allowed to “separately” process the
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages from time
t^{\prime}
because, after all these
\mathtt{INSTALL}
messages are processed, we obtain the exact same system state as we would obtain if all the messages were processed “together”.

Lemma 41
.

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{set},\omega]\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
; let that time be
t^{\prime}>t
.
Then,
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v)
or
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

18
,
v
is a valid view.
Moreover,
\mathit{preconditions}(v,t^{\prime}-1)=\top
(since
t^{\prime}
is the first time after time
t
at which an
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message is obtained and by
Lemmas

34
,
37
and
40
).
By safety of
\mathit{vg}(v)
, we have that
(v^{*},\emptyset,\epsilon^{*})\in\Lambda_{t^{\prime}-1}^{v}
or
(v^{*},\mathit{set},\epsilon^{*})\in\Lambda_{t^{\prime}-1}^{v}
.
If
(v^{*},\emptyset,\epsilon^{*})\in\Lambda_{t^{\prime}-1}^{v}
, then
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
(by
Lemma

35
).
If
(v^{*},\mathit{set},\epsilon^{*})\in\Lambda_{t^{\prime}-1}^{v}
, then
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v)
(by
Lemma

34
).
Therefore, the lemma holds.
∎

Next, we prove some properties of
\mathit{set}
.

Lemma 42
.

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{set},\omega]\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
; let that time be
t^{\prime}>t
.
Then, (1)
\mathit{set}\neq\emptyset
, (2)
\mathit{set}
is a sequence, and (3)
\mathit{set}.\mathtt{follows}(v)=\top
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

18
,
v
is a valid view.
Moreover,
\mathit{preconditions}(v,t^{\prime}-1)=\top
(since
t^{\prime}
is the first time after time
t
at which an
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message is obtained and by
Lemmas

34
,
37
and
40
).
Hence, the lemma follows from validity of
\mathit{vg}(v)
.
∎

Finally, we prove that all well-founded views at time
t
are comparable.
This lemma is the crucial ingredient for proving the view comparability property (see
).

Lemma 43
.

Let
v,v^{\prime}\in V(t)
.
Then, either
v\subseteq v^{\prime}
or
v\supset v^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
v=v^{\prime}
, the lemma holds.
Otherwise, we consider four possible cases:

1.

\alpha_{t}(v)=\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
: In this case, the lemma follows from
Lemma

27
.

2.

\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
and
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\bot
:
Let
v^{\prime}
be the auxiliary view for a view
v_{i}
at time
t
(such view
v_{i}
exists due to
Lemma

29
).
According to
Lemma

27
,
v\subseteq v_{i}
or
v\supset v_{i}
.
We analyze all three possibilities:

•

v=v_{i}
: In this case, the lemma holds since
v\subset v^{\prime}
(by
Lemma

30
).

•

v\subset v_{i}
: We have that
v_{i}\subset v^{\prime}
(by
Lemma

30
).
Hence,
v\subset v^{\prime}
and the lemma holds.

•

v\supset v_{i}
: Let
v_{i}
lead to a view
v^{\prime\prime}
at time
t
(such view
v^{\prime\prime}
exists due to
Lemmas

25
and
28
).
Because of
Lemma

27
and
Lemma

32
, we have that
v^{\prime\prime}\subseteq v
.
We conclude that
v^{\prime}\subset v
since
v^{\prime}\subset v^{\prime\prime}
(by
Lemma

31
).

3.

\alpha_{t}(v)=\bot
and
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
: Analogous to the previous case.

4.

\alpha_{t}(v)=\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\bot
: Let
v
be an auxiliary view for
v_{i}
and let
v^{\prime}
be an auxiliary view for
v_{i}^{\prime}
(by
Lemma

29
).
According to
Lemma

27
, we have three possibilities to examine:

•

v_{i}=v_{i}^{\prime}
: By
Definition

4
, we have that
\mathit{seq},\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}=...\to v\to...
and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=...\to v^{\prime}\to...
.
The lemma follows from
Lemma

20
,
1
and the definition of a sequence.

•

v_{i}\subset v_{i}^{\prime}
: Let
v_{i}
lead to a view
v^{\prime\prime}
at time
t
(such view
v^{\prime\prime}
exists by
Lemmas

25
and
28
).
Because of
Lemma

27
and
Lemma

32
, we have that
v^{\prime\prime}\subseteq v_{i}^{\prime}
.
We conclude that
v\subset v^{\prime}
since
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}
(by
Lemma

31
).

•

v_{i}\supset v_{i}^{\prime}
: Analogous to the case
v_{i}\subset v_{i}^{\prime}
.

The lemma holds since it holds in all four possibilities.
∎

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{set},\omega]
be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
.
Let that happen at time
t^{\prime}
.
The following lemma proves that
\mathit{set}
comes from a view that is installable at time
t^{\prime}
.

Lemma 44
.

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{seq}=v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},\omega]\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
, where
y\geq 1
, be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
; let that be at time
t^{\prime}>t
.
Then:

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})
, where
v^{\prime}\in V(t^{\prime})
,
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\top
and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t^{\prime})
and
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

If
\alpha_{t}(v)=\top
, then
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v)=\top
(since
\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{*}(t^{\prime})
).
Moreover, we have that
\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v)
, which concludes the lemma.

Let
\alpha_{t}(v)=\bot
.
According to
Lemma

41
,
\mathit{seq}\in\beta_{t}(v)
.
Since
5
holds at time
t
, we conclude that:

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
, where
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
,
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
and
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

At time
t^{\prime}
, the previous statements still hold (since
\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{*}(t^{\prime})
).
Moreover,
\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v)
, which concludes the lemma.
∎

Finally, we show that all invariants are preserved after a new
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message is obtained.

Theorem 17
.

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{seq}=v^{\prime},\omega]\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
; let that be at time
t^{\prime}>t
.
Let
v^{\prime}\notin V(t)
.
Then, all invariants hold at time
t^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We prove the preservation of invariants one by one.

1
:

1
holds for
v^{\prime}
at time
t^{\prime}
since
\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\emptyset
.
Let us prove this statement.
By contradiction, suppose that a process has obtained an
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message associated with view
v^{\prime}
before time
t^{\prime}
.
Since
v^{\prime}\notin V(t)
, we know that
v^{\prime}
is not a valid view (by
Lemma

17
).
However, since
v^{\prime}\in V(t^{\prime})
,
v^{\prime}
is a valid view according to
Lemma

18
.
Thus, contradiction.

The invariant holds for all views
v_{t}\in V(t)
, where
v_{t}\neq v
, because
\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{t})=\rho_{t}(v_{t})
and the invariant holds at time
t
.
Finally, the invariant holds for
v
at time
t^{\prime}
because of
Lemma

42
and the fact that the invariant holds at time
t
.

2
:

We conclude that
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\top
.
By
Lemma

44
, there exists a view
v^{*}\in V(t^{\prime})
such that:

•

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t^{\prime})
and
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime}
.

Hence,
2
holds for
v^{\prime}
at time
t^{\prime}
.

Since
v^{\prime}\notin V(t)
and
2
holds at time
t
, we conclude that
2
holds for all views
v_{t}\in V(t)
.
Thus,
2
holds at time
t^{\prime}
.

3
:

Since
v^{*}\in V(t)
and
3
holds at time
t
, we know that
\mathit{genesis}\subseteq v^{*}
.
We know that
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})
.
Since
1
holds at time
t^{\prime}
, we know that
v^{*}\subset v^{\prime}
.
Hence,
\mathit{genesis}\subset v^{\prime}
, which proves the invariant preservation at time
t^{\prime}
.

4
:

Since
v^{\prime}\notin V(t)
,
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\top
and
4
holds at time
t
,
4
holds at time
t^{\prime}
.

5
:

Since
v^{\prime}\notin V(t)
,
\beta_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\emptyset
and
5
holds at time
t
,
5
holds at time
t^{\prime}
.
∎

Now, we analyze the second possibility.

Theorem 18
.

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{seq}=v^{\prime},\omega]\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
; let that be at time
t^{\prime}>t
.
Let
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
.
Then, all invariants hold at time
t^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We separate the proof into two cases.

(1) Let
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
.

1
:

1
holds for all views
v_{t}\in V(t)\setminus\{v\}
at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.
The invariant holds for
v
at time
t^{\prime}
because of
Lemma

42
and the fact that the invariant holds at time
t
.

2
:

Since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, all views installable at time
t
are installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
The fact that
2
holds at time
t
implies that the invariant holds at time
t^{\prime}
.

3
:

Since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, all views installable at time
t
are installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Hence,
3
holds at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.

4
:

Since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, all views installable at time
t
are installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Hence, the invariant holds at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.

5
:

Since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, all views installable at time
t
are installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Moreover,
\beta_{t^{\prime}}(v_{t})=\beta_{t}(v_{t})
, for every view
v_{t}\in V(t)
(note that
V(t^{\prime})=V(t)
).
Hence, the invariant holds at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.

(2) Let
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\bot
.

1
:

1
holds for all views
v_{t}\in V(t)\setminus\{v\}
at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.
The invariant holds for
v
at time
t^{\prime}
because of
Lemma

42
and the fact that the invariant holds at time
t
.

2
:

By
Lemma

44
, there exists a view
v^{*}\in V(t^{\prime})
such that:

•

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t^{\prime})
and
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime}
.

Hence,
2
holds for
v^{\prime}
at time
t^{\prime}
.

Consider now a view
v_{\mathit{ins}}\in V(t)
such that (1)
v_{\mathit{ins}}\neq\mathit{genesis}
, and (2)
\alpha_{t}(v_{\mathit{ins}})=\top
.
Since
2
holds at time
t
and
v_{\mathit{ins}}\neq\mathit{genesis}
, there is a view
v_{\mathit{lead}}
that leads to
v_{\mathit{ins}}
at time
t
(by
Lemma

22
).
By
Definition

3
, we have:

•

\alpha_{t}(v_{\mathit{lead}})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}_{\mathit{ins}}\in\rho_{t}(v_{\mathit{lead}})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{\mathit{ins}}=v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}\to v_{\mathit{ins}}
and
y\geq 0
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1}^{\prime},...,v_{y}^{\prime}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1}^{\prime},...,v_{y}^{\prime}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}\to v_{\mathit{ins}}
.

If
v^{\prime}\notin\mathit{seq}_{\mathit{ins}}
,
2
is satisfied for
v_{\mathit{ins}}
at time
t^{\prime}
.

Otherwise, let
\mathit{seq}_{\mathit{ins}}=v_{1}^{\prime\prime}\to...\to v_{z}^{\prime\prime}\to v^{\prime}\to v_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\to...\to v_{q}^{\prime\prime\prime}\to v_{\mathit{ins}}
, where
z\geq 0
and
q\geq 0
.
The following holds at time
t^{\prime}
:

•

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}_{v}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{v}=v_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\to...\to v_{q}^{\prime\prime\prime}\to v_{\mathit{ins}}
(recall that
\mathit{seq}_{v}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
), and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime},...,v_{q}^{\prime\prime\prime}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t^{\prime})
and
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime},...,v_{q}^{\prime\prime\prime}\}
,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}_{i}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i}^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}_{i}=v_{i+1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\to...\to v_{q}^{\prime\prime\prime}\to v_{\mathit{ins}}
.

Therefore,
2
is, even in this case, satisfied for
\mathit{v}_{\mathit{ins}}
at time
t^{\prime}
.

3
:

Since
v^{*}\in V(t)
and
3
holds at time
t
,
\mathit{genesis}\subseteq v^{*}
.
Moreover,
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})
.
Since
1
holds at time
t^{\prime}
,
v^{*}\subset v^{\prime}
.
Hence,
\mathit{genesis}\subset v^{\prime}
, which proves the invariant preservation at time
t^{\prime}
.

4
:

Consider a view
v_{\mathit{aux}}\in V(t)
such that (1)
\alpha_{t}(v_{\mathit{aux}})=\bot
, and (2)
v_{\mathit{aux}}\neq v^{\prime}
.
Since all invariants hold at time
t
, we have (by
Definition

4
):

•

\alpha_{t}(v_{\mathit{ins}})=\top
, for some view
v_{\mathit{ins}}\in V(t)
, and

•

\mathit{seq}_{\mathit{aux}}\in\rho_{t}(v_{\mathit{ins}})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{\mathit{aux}}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v_{\mathit{aux}}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
,
x\geq 0
and
y\geq 1
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v_{\mathit{aux}}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

If
v^{\prime}\notin\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
4
is satisfied for
v_{\mathit{aux}}
at time
t^{\prime}
.

Otherwise, let
\mathit{seq}_{\mathit{aux}}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{z-1}\to v^{\prime}\to v_{z+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v_{\mathit{aux}}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
, where
z\geq 0
.
The following holds at time
t^{\prime}
:

•

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}_{v^{\prime}}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{v^{\prime}}=v_{z+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v_{\mathit{aux}}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
(recall that
\mathit{seq}_{v^{\prime}}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
), and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{z+1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t^{\prime})
and
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{z+1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v_{\mathit{aux}}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

Therefore,
4
is, even in this case, satisfied for
\mathit{v}_{\mathit{aux}}
at time
t^{\prime}
.

5
:

We have that
\beta_{t^{\prime}}(v_{t})=\beta_{t}(v_{t})
, for every view
v_{t}\in V(t^{\prime})
(recall that
V(t^{\prime})=V(t)
).
Consider a view
v^{**}\in V(t^{\prime})
such that
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t}(v^{**})
and
\mathit{set}=\{v_{1}^{\prime},...,v_{y}^{\prime}\}
, where
y\geq 1
.
We know that
\mathit{set}\in\beta_{t^{\prime}}(v^{**})
.
Since
5
holds at time
t
, we have the following:

•

\mathit{seq}^{**}\in\rho_{t}(v_{\mathit{ins}})
, where
\alpha_{t}(v_{\mathit{ins}})=\top
and
\mathit{seq}^{**}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{**}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t)
and
\alpha_{t}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{**}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

Now, if
v^{\prime}\notin\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
5
is satisfied for
v^{**}
at time
t^{\prime}
.

Otherwise, let
\mathit{seq}^{**}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{z-1}\to v^{\prime}\to v_{z+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{**}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
, where
z\geq 0
.
The following holds at time
t^{\prime}
:

•

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}_{v^{\prime}}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{v^{\prime}}=v_{z+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{**}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
(recall that
\mathit{seq}_{v^{\prime}}\in\rho_{t}(v^{\prime})
), and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{z+1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t^{\prime})
and
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{z+1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{**}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

Therefore,
5
is, even in this case, satisfied for
\mathit{v}^{**}
at time
t^{\prime}
.
∎

Next, we analyze the third possible case.

Theorem 19
.

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{seq}=v^{\prime}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},\omega]\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
; let that be at time
t^{\prime}>t
.
Let
v^{\prime}\notin V(t)
12
12

12

Observe that
v^{\prime}
is the destination of the message since
\mathit{seq}
is a sequence (by
Lemma

42
) and
v^{\prime}
is the smallest view of the sequence.
and
y\geq 1
.
Then, all invariants hold at time
t^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We prove the preservation of the invariants one by one.

1
:

1
holds for
v^{\prime}
at time
t^{\prime}
since
\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\emptyset
.
Let us prove this statement.
By contradiction, suppose that a process has obtained an
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message associated with view
v^{\prime}
before time
t^{\prime}
.
Since
v^{\prime}\notin V(t)
, we know that
v^{\prime}
is not a valid view (by
Lemma

17
).
However, since
v^{\prime}\in V(t^{\prime})
,
v^{\prime}
is a valid view according to
Lemma

18
.
Thus, contradiction.

The invariant holds for all views
v_{t}\in V(t)
, where
v_{t}\neq v
, because
\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{t})=\rho_{t}(v_{t})
and the invariant holds at time
t
.
Finally, the invariant holds for
v
at time
t^{\prime}
because of
Lemma

42
and the fact that the invariant holds at time
t
.

2
:

The set of installable views at time
t^{\prime}
is identical to the set of installable views at time
t
.
Thus, the invariant is preserved since it is satisfied at time
t
.

3
:

The set of installable views at time
t^{\prime}
is identical to the set of installable views at time
t
.
Thus, the invariant is preserved since it is satisfied at time
t
.

4
:

By
Lemma

44
, there exists a view
v^{*}\in V(t^{\prime})
such that:

•

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t^{\prime})
and
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

Hence,
4
is satisfied at time
t^{\prime}
for
v^{\prime}
.
Moreover, since the set of installable views at time
t^{\prime}
is identical to the set of installable views at time
t
and
4
holds at time
t
, the invariant is preserved at time
t^{\prime}
.

5
:

We have that
\beta_{t^{\prime}}(v^{\prime})=\{v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}\}
.
By
Lemma

44
, there exists a view
v^{*}\in V(t)
such that:

•

\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})=\top
, and

•

\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v^{*})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},(x\geq 0)
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
v_{i}\in V(t^{\prime})
and
\alpha_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})=\bot
, and

•

for every
v_{i}\in\{v_{1},...,v_{x}\}
,
\mathit{seq}_{i}\in\rho_{t^{\prime}}(v_{i})
, where
\mathit{seq}_{i}=v_{i+1}\to...\to v_{x}\to v^{\prime}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime}
.

Hence,
5
is satisfied for
v^{\prime}
at time
t^{\prime}
.

For every view
v_{t}\in V(t)
,
\beta_{t^{\prime}}(v_{t})=\beta_{t}(v_{t})
.
Therefore,
5
is satisfied for
v_{t}
at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
and the set of installable views at time
t^{\prime}
is identical to the set of installable views at time
t
.
∎

Lastly, we analyze the fourth possible case.

Theorem 20
.

Let
[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{seq}=v^{\prime}\to v_{1}^{\prime}\to...\to v_{y}^{\prime},\omega]\notin\mathcal{I}^{*}(t)
be the first
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message obtained by a (correct or faulty) process after time
t
such that
v\in V(t)
; let that be at time
t^{\prime}>t
.
Let
v^{\prime}\in V(t)
13
13

13

Note that
v^{\prime}
is the destination of the message since
\mathit{seq}
is a sequence (by
Lemma

42
) and
v^{\prime}
is the smallest view of the sequence.
and
y\geq 1
.
Then, all invariants hold at time
t^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We distinguish two cases:

(1) Let
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
.

1
:

1
holds for all views
v_{t}\in V(t)\setminus\{v\}
at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.
The invariant holds for
v
at time
t^{\prime}
because of
Lemma

42
and the fact that the invariant holds at time
t
.

2
:

Since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, all views installable at time
t
are installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
The fact that
2
holds at time
t
implies that the invariant holds at time
t^{\prime}
.

3
:

Since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, all views installable at time
t
are installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Hence,
3
holds at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.

4
:

Since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, all views installable at time
t
are installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Hence, the invariant holds at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.

5
:

Since
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\top
, all views installable at time
t
are installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Moreover,
\beta_{t^{\prime}}(v_{t})=\beta_{t}(v_{t})
, for every view
v_{t}\in V(t)\setminus{\{v^{\prime}\}}
(note that
V(t^{\prime})=V(t)
).
Hence, the invariant holds at time
t^{\prime}
for
v_{t}
since it holds at time
t
.
Furthermore,
5
holds for
v^{\prime}
at time
t^{\prime}
because of
Lemma

44
and the fact that the invariant holds at time
t
.
Thus, the invariant is preserved at time
t^{\prime}
.

(2) Let
\alpha_{t}(v^{\prime})=\bot
.

1
:

1
holds for all views
v_{t}\in V(t)\setminus\{v\}
at time
t^{\prime}
since it holds at time
t
.
The invariant holds for
v
at time
t^{\prime}
because of
Lemma

42
and the fact that the invariant holds at time
t
.

2
:

The set of views installable at time
t
is identical to the set of views installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Given that the invariant holds at time
t
, the invariant is preserved at time
t^{\prime}
.

3
:

The set of views installable at time
t
is identical to the set of views installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Given that the invariant holds at time
t
, the invariant is preserved at time
t^{\prime}
.

4
:

The set of views installable at time
t
is identical to the set of views installable at time
t^{\prime}
.
Given that the invariant holds at time
t
, the invariant is preserved at time
t^{\prime}
.

5
:

We have that
\beta_{t^{\prime}}(v_{t})=\beta_{t}(v_{t})
, for every view
v_{t}\in V(t^{\prime})\setminus{\{v^{\prime}\}}
(note that
V(t^{\prime})=V(t)
).
Therefore, the invariant is ensured at time
t^{\prime}
for
v_{t}
since it holds at time
t
.
Moreover,
5
is ensured for
v^{\prime}
at time
t^{\prime}
because of
Lemma

44
and the fact that the invariant holds at time
t
.
Thus, the invariant is preserved at time
t^{\prime}
.
∎

Epilogue.

We have shown that, at all times, all the invariants we have introduced hold.
Now, we give some results that represent consequences of the introduced invariants.

We start by proving that all valid views belong to
V(\infty)
.

Lemma 45

(Valid Views are in
V(\infty)
)
.

Let a view
v
be valid.
Then,
v\in V(\infty)
.

Proof.

Follows from
Definition

1
and the definition of
V(\infty)
.
∎

Since all invariants hold at all times, we have the following for each valid view
v\in V(\infty)
:

•

If
\mathit{set}\in\rho_{\infty}(v)
, then (1)
\mathit{set}\neq\emptyset
, (2)
\mathit{set}
is a sequence, and (3)
\mathit{set}.\mathtt{follows}(v)=\top
(due to the fact that
1
holds at time
\infty
).

•

If
\alpha_{\infty}(v)=\bot
and
\mathit{seq}\in\rho_{\infty}(v)
, where
\mathit{set}=v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}
(
x\geq 1
), then
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}\in\rho_{\infty}(v^{\prime})
, where
\mathit{seq}^{\prime}=...\to v\to v_{1}\to...\to v_{x}
and
v
is an auxiliary view for
v^{\prime}
at time
\infty
(due to
Lemmas

39
and
41
).

•

\mathit{preconditions}(v,t)=\top
, for any time
t
(this follows from the fact that
\mathit{preconditions}(v,\infty)=\top
, which is a result of
Lemmas

34
,
37
and
40
).

Next, any obtained
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message associated with a valid view “carries” a sequence that follows the associated view.

Lemma 46
.

Let a (correct or faulty) process obtain the
m=[\mathtt{INSTALL},\mathit{source},\mathit{set},\omega]
message, where
\mathit{source}=m.\mathtt{source()}
is a valid view.
Then, (1)
\mathit{set}\neq\emptyset
, (2)
\mathit{set}
is a sequence, and (3)
\mathit{set}.\mathtt{follows}(\mathit{source})=\top
.

Proof.

Since
\mathit{source}
is a valid view,
\mathit{source}\in V(\infty)
(by
Lemma

45
).
Moreover,
m\in\mathcal{I}^{*}(\infty)
.
Hence,
\mathit{set}\in\rho_{\infty}(\mathit{source})
.
Therefore, the lemma holds since
1
holds at time
\infty
.
∎

Let
m=[\mathtt{INSTALL},v,\mathit{seq},\omega]
, where
v
is a valid view.
According to
Lemma

46
,
\mathit{seq}\neq\emptyset
,
\mathit{seq}
is a sequence and
\mathit{seq}.\mathtt{follows}(v)=\top
.
Moreover,
m.\mathtt{destination()}=\mathit{seq}.\mathtt{first()}
and
m.\mathtt{tail()}=\mathit{seq}\setminus\{\mathit{seq}.\mathtt{first()}\}
.

The next lemma proves that a view
v
leads to a view
v^{\prime}
at time
\infty
if and only if
v^{\prime}
is the smallest installable view greater than
v
.

Lemma 47
.

Let
v,v^{\prime}\in V(\infty)
such that
\alpha_{\infty}(v)=\alpha_{\infty}(v^{\prime})=\top
and
v\subset v^{\prime}
.
Then,
v
leads to
v^{\prime}
at time
\infty
if and only if there does not exist a view
v^{\prime\prime}\in V(\infty)
such that
\alpha_{\infty}(v^{\prime\prime})=\top
and
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}\subset v^{\prime}
.

Proof.

In order to prove the lemma, we prove the both directions of the statement.

First, if
v
leads to
v^{\prime}
, then there does not exist a view
v^{\prime\prime}
such that
\alpha_{\infty}(v^{\prime\prime})=\top
and
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}\subset v^{\prime}
.
This statement is true due to
Lemma

32
.

Now, suppose that such view
v^{\prime\prime}
does not exist.
We need to prove that
v
leads to
v^{\prime}
at time
\infty
.
By contradiction, suppose that
v
does not lead to
v^{\prime}
at time
\infty
.
Since
v\subset v^{\prime}
,
v
leads to a view
v_{i}\neq v^{\prime}
at time
\infty
(by
Lemmas

25
and
28
).
By
Lemma

21
,
v\subset v_{i}
.
By
Lemmas

27
and
32
,
v_{i}\subseteq v^{\prime}
.
Since
v_{i}\neq v^{\prime}
,
v_{i}\subset v^{\prime}
.
However, this contradicts the fact that an installable view greater than
v
and smaller than
v^{\prime}
does not exist.
Thus, the lemma holds.
∎

Similarly to
Lemma

47
, a view
v^{\prime}
is an auxiliary view for a view
v
at time
\infty
if and only if
v
is the greatest installable view smaller than
v^{\prime}
.

Lemma 48
.

Let
v,v^{\prime}\in V(\infty)
such that
\alpha_{\infty}(v)=\top
,
\alpha_{\infty}(v^{\prime})=\bot
and
v\subset v^{\prime}
.
Then,
v^{\prime}
is an auxiliary view for
v
at time
\infty
if and only if there does not exist a view
v^{\prime\prime}\in V(\infty)
such that
\alpha_{\infty}(v^{\prime\prime})=\top
and
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}\subset v^{\prime}
.

Proof.

In order to prove the lemma, we prove the both directions of the statement.

First, if
v^{\prime}
is an auxiliary view for
v
at time
\infty
, then there does not exist a view
v^{\prime\prime}
such that
\alpha_{\infty}(v^{\prime\prime})=\top
and
v\subset v^{\prime\prime}\subset v^{\prime}
.
By contradiction, let there exist such view
v^{\prime\prime}
.
By
Lemmas

25
and
28
,
v
leads to a view
v_{i}
at time
\infty
.
Moreover,
v_{i}\subseteq v^{\prime\prime}
(by
Lemmas

27
and
32
).
Since
v^{\prime}
is an auxiliary view for
v
at time
\infty
,
v^{\prime}\subset v_{i}
(by
Lemma

31
).
Hence,
v^{\prime}\subset v^{\prime\prime}
, which contradicts that
v^{\prime\prime}\subset v^{\prime}
.
This direction of the statement is satisfied.

Now, suppose that such view
v^{\prime\prime}
does not exist.
We need to prove that
v^{\prime}
is an auxiliary view for
v
at time
\infty
.
By contradiction, suppose that
v^{\prime}
is not an auxiliary view for
v
at time
\infty
.
Let
v^{\prime}
be an auxiliary view for a view
v^{*}\neq v
(such view
v^{*}
exists due to
Lemma

29
).
By
Lemma

30
,
v^{*}\subset v^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

27
, either
v\subseteq v^{*}
or
v\supset v^{*}
.
Since
v\neq v^{*}
and no installable view “between”
v
and
v^{\prime}
exists,
v^{*}\subset v
.
Let
v^{*}
lead to a view
v_{i}
(by
Lemmas

25
and
28
).
By
Lemmas

27
and
32
,
v_{i}\subseteq v
.
By
Lemma

31
,
v^{\prime}\subset v_{i}
.
Hence,
v^{\prime}\subset v
, which represents contradiction with
v\subset v^{\prime}
.
Thus, the lemma holds.
∎

The next lemma shows that all valid views are greater than or equal to
\mathit{genesis}
.

Lemma 49
.

Let a view
v
be valid.
Then,
\mathit{genesis}\subseteq v
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

45
, we know that
v,\mathit{genesis}\in V(\infty)
.
By
Lemma

43
, we know that either
\mathit{genesis}\subseteq v
or
\mathit{genesis}\supset v
.
For the sake of contradiction, let
v\subset\mathit{genesis}
.

Since
3
holds at time
\infty
,
\alpha_{\infty}(v)=\bot
.
Therefore,
v
is an auxiliary view for some view
v^{*}
at time
\infty
(by
Lemma

29
).
By
Lemma

30
,
v^{*}\subset v
, which implies that
v^{*}\subset\mathit{genesis}
.
However, this is impossible due to
3
.
The lemma holds.
∎

The next lemma shows that views that “belong” to a view-path are ordered.

Lemma 50
.

Let
\mathit{path}=[m_{1},...,m_{k}]
, where
k\geq 1
, be a view-path.
Then:

1.

m_{i-1}.\mathtt{source()}\subset m_{i}.\mathtt{source()}
, for every
i\in[2,k]
, and

2.

m_{k}.\mathtt{source()}\subset m_{k}.\mathtt{destination()}
.

Proof.

Note that
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{views()}\subseteq V(\infty)
(by
Lemma

45
).
Since
m_{i}.\mathtt{source()}=m_{i-1}.\mathtt{destination()}
, for every
i\in[2,k]
(see
), the first claim of the lemma follows from
1
.
Moreover,
1
ensures that the second claim of the lemma is true, as well.
∎

Next, we show that it is impossible that
(+,r)\notin v
and
(-,r)\in v
, where
v
is a valid view and
r
is a server.

Lemma 51
.

Let
v
be a valid view such that
(-,r)\in v
, where
r
is a server.
Then,
(+,r)\in v
.

Proof.

By contradiction, let
(-,r)\in v
and
(+,r)\notin v
.
First,
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
due to the fact that
\mathit{genesis}
does not contain “negative” changes (see
Appendix

B
, paragraph “Constants”).
Thus, there exists a view-path
\mathit{path}\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{*}(\infty)
such that
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination()}=v
(by
Definition

1
).

By
Lemma

50
,
v^{\prime}\subset v
, where
v^{\prime}=m.\mathtt{source()}
,
v=m.\mathtt{destination()}
and
m
is the last message of
\mathit{path}
.
Thus,
(+,r)\notin v^{\prime}
, which means that the membership validity property of
\mathit{vg}(v^{\prime})
is violated (recall that
v^{\prime}
is a valid view, by
Lemma

19
, and
\mathit{preconditions}(v^{\prime},\infty)=\top
).
Hence, the lemma holds.
∎

We now show that there are finitely many valid views (see
).

Theorem 21

(Finitely Many Valid Views)
.

There exist only finitely many valid views.

Proof.

Consider any valid view
v
.
We prove that
|C_{v}^{+}=\{(+,r)\,|\,(+,r)\in v\}|<\infty
.
We distinguish two possible cases:

•

Let
v=\mathit{genesis}
.
In this case,
|C_{v}^{+}|<\infty
due to the definition of
\mathit{genesis}
(see
Appendix

B
, paragraph “Constants”).

•

Let
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
.
By
Definition

1
, there exists a view-path
\mathit{path}\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{*}(\infty)
such that
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination()}=v
.
Let
m\in\mathit{path}
be the
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message such that
m.\mathtt{destination()}=v
; note that
m.\mathtt{source()}
is a valid view.
Now, for every
(+,r)\in C_{v}^{+}
, the
\mathtt{JOIN}
message is sent by
r
(see
); hence,
r
has previously started.
Due to the assumption that only finitely many servers start,
|C_{v}^{+}|<\infty
.

Now, let
C_{v}^{-}=\{(-,r)\,|\,(-,r)\in v\}
.
Because of
Lemma

51
and
|C_{v}^{+}|<\infty
, we conclude that
|C_{v}^{-}|<\infty
.
Therefore,
|v|<\infty
.
Recall that every valid view belongs to
V(\infty)
(by
Lemma

45
).
Since all valid views have finitely many changes, all valid views belong to
V(\infty)
and all views from
V(\infty)
are comparable (by
Lemma

43
), the theorem holds.
∎

Moreover, we prove that there are finitely many sequences in
\rho_{\infty}(v)
, where
v
is a valid view.

Lemma 52

(Finitely Many Sequences)
.

Let view
v
be valid.
Then,
|\rho_{\infty}(v)|<\infty
.

Proof.

Follows directly from the bounded decisions property of
\mathit{vg}(v)
.
∎

For the sake of brevity, we might use the result of
Lemma

52
without explicitly stating it in the following.

F-B
2
Safety Properties

We start by proving the view comparability property specified in
.

Theorem 22

(View Comparability)
.

View comparability is satisfied.

Proof.

All valid views belong to
V(\infty)
(by
Lemma

45
).
Hence, the theorem follows from
Lemma

43
.
∎

The direct consequence of the view comparability property is that the
\mathit{history}
variable (initialized at line
6
of
) of a correct server is a sequence.
The indirect consequences of the property are:

\mathit{sequence}

) of a correct server contains sequences.

2.

) of a correct server is a sequence (or

).

We are ready to prove the join safety property (see
Section

C-C
, paragraph “Properties of Carbon”).

Theorem 23

(Join Safety)
.

Join safety is satisfied.

Proof.

Let a server
r
join and let
r\notin\mathit{genesis}.\mathtt{members()}
.
We prove that
r
is voted in, i.e., that a process obtains a voting proof
\sigma_{v}
such that
\mathtt{verify\_voting}(\text{``add server }r\text{''},\sigma_{v})=\top
.

Since
r
joins and
r\notin\mathit{genesis}.\mathtt{members()}
, there exists a correct server that executes line
33
of
(if
r
is correct, then
r
itself executes this line; see
Section

C-C
, paragraph “Validators”) or line
59
of
(if
r
is faulty, a correct server executes this line).
Hence, there exists a valid view
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
such that
r\in v.\mathtt{members()}
.

Since
v\neq\mathit{genesis}
is valid, there exists a view-path
\mathit{path}\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{*}(\infty)
such that
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination()}=v
(by
Definition

1
).
All views that belong to
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{views()}
are valid (by
Lemma

19
).
Hence, there exists an
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message
m
such that
m.\mathtt{source()}
is a valid view and
r\in m.\mathtt{destination()}.\mathtt{members()}
.
Finally, the theorem holds because the evidence handed to the view generator primitive contains
\mathtt{JOIN}
messages with the voting proofs for every
(+,r^{\prime})
“proposed” change (by line
16
or line
52
of
), where
r^{\prime}
is a server.
∎

The next theorem proves the leave safety property of Carbon (see
Section

C-C
, paragraph “Properties of Carbon”).

Theorem 24

(Leave Safety)
.

Leave safety is satisfied.

Proof.

Let a correct server
r
leave.
Hence,
r
executes line
54
of
.
Therefore, there exist valid views
v_{1}
and
v_{2}
such that (1)
r\in v_{1}.\mathtt{members()}
, and (2)
r\notin v_{2}.\mathtt{members()}
(by line
3
of
).

Since
r\in v_{1}.\mathtt{members()}
,
(+,r)\in v_{1}
(see
).
Moreover, since
v_{1}\subset v_{2}
and
r\notin v_{2}.\mathtt{members()}
,
(+,r),(-,r)\in v_{2}
.
Hence,
v_{2}\neq\mathit{genesis}
(since
\mathit{genesis}
does not contain “negative” changes; see
Appendix

B
, paragraph “Constants”).
By
Definition

1
, there exists a view-path
\mathit{path}\subseteq\mathcal{I}^{*}(\infty)
such that
\mathit{path}.\mathtt{destination()}=v_{2}
.
Similarly to the proof of join safety (
Theorem

23
), there exists an
\mathtt{INSTALL}
message
m
such that
m.\mathtt{source()}
is a valid view and
(-,r)\in m.\mathtt{destination()}
.
Furthermore, the evidence handed to the view generator primitive contains either voting proofs or
\mathtt{LEAVE}
messages for every
(-,r^{\prime})
“proposed” change (by line

or line

of
), where

is a server.
Therefore,
r
either sent the

Finally, if

\mathtt{LEAVE}
message, then
r
has previously requested to leave (because the

of
).
The theorem holds.
∎
